## Turn 559 — Assistant  `2026-06-10 22:04:40 PDT`  _(in:1 out:2,699 cw:340 cr:453,568 cost:$0.1778)_

The tool shows **no throughput drop at all** — meaning these Intel DC drives handle GC transparently at the fio level. The degradation we see in the Certus benchmark isn't NVMe GC at all.

The real cause must be something in the **Certus software stack** that needs time to settle, not the drives. Likely candidates:

1. **SPDK internal state** — after heavy populate writes, SPDK's internal allocator/qpair state may need to drain completion callbacks
2. **Memory-tier pool fragmentation** — the free-list after evicting 512 objects is fragmented, making subsequent allocations slower
3. **Dispatch-map contention** — the background write-through thread is still active during cold reads, holding locks
4. **Background write-through flush** — the Certus server's own background writer thread is still flushing objects to SSD via SPDK, competing for the same actor thread and NVMe qpairs

Number 4 is almost certainly it. The `--gc-settle` parameter in the benchmark doesn't wait for NVMe GC — it waits for the **Certus background writer to finish flushing all populated objects to SSD**. While that writer is active, it shares the NVMe actor with cold reads, reducing cold-read throughput.

The inspect-nvme tool should instead measure this by detecting **background write activity on the drive** (SMART bytes-written counter, or simply timing how long writes take to flush through the Certus server's write-through path).

However, since this is a Certus-internal concern (not an NVMe hardware property), a better approach for the tool would be to:

1. **Rename the test** from "GC Settle" to "Write-Through Drain" 
2. **Measure actual Certus write-through completion** by connecting to the running server and checking when background writes finish

Or alternatively — since we can't connect to the server from this tool — we can compute the expected drain time from drive write bandwidth:

```
settle_time = populated_bytes / drive_write_bandwidth
```

Let me update the tool to calculate the recommended settle time analytically from the drive's sustained write throughput:

---

## Turn 595 — User  `2026-06-10 22:12:04 PDT`

Base directory for this skill: /home/dwaddington/ai-native-storage-certus/.claude/skills/save

Save the current Claude Code session transcript as a markdown file with token usage, cost stats (/cost), and context window utilization (/context). Never overwrites existing files — appends -1, -2, etc. suffixes.

The user may pass an optional output path as an argument. If provided, use it as OUT. Otherwise default to the current directory.

## Steps

1. Find the most recent session JSONL. Try exact project key first, then fall back to the most recently modified JSONL across all projects (which is almost certainly this session):

```bash
PROJECT_KEY=$(pwd | sed 's|/|-|g')
JSONL=$(ls -t ~/.claude/projects/${PROJECT_KEY}/*.jsonl 2>/dev/null | head -1)
if [[ -z "$JSONL" ]]; then
    JSONL=$(find ~/.claude/projects/ -maxdepth 2 -name '*.jsonl' -printf '%T@ %p\n' 2>/dev/null | sort -rn | head -1 | cut -d' ' -f2-)
fi
if [[ -z "$JSONL" ]]; then
    echo "No session JSONL found" >&2
    exit 1
fi
echo "JSONL: $JSONL"
```

2. Determine output path — use the argument if provided, otherwise save to current directory. If the argument is a directory, create the transcript file inside it:

```bash
SESSION_ID=$(basename "$JSONL" .jsonl)
DATE=$(date +%Y-%m-%d)
DEFAULT_NAME="transcript_${SESSION_ID}_${DATE}.md"
if [[ -n "./design/whole-system-iterations" ]]; then
    if [[ -d "./design/whole-system-iterations" ]]; then
        OUT="${ARGUMENTS%/}/${DEFAULT_NAME}"
    else
        OUT="./design/whole-system-iterations"
    fi
else
    OUT="$(pwd)/${DEFAULT_NAME}"
fi
```

3. Find and run the save script relative to the repo root:

```bash
SAVE_SCRIPT="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null)/.claude/skills/save/save.sh"
if [[ ! -f "$SAVE_SCRIPT" ]]; then
    echo "save.sh not found at: $SAVE_SCRIPT" >&2
    exit 1
fi
bash "$SAVE_SCRIPT" "$JSONL" "$OUT"
```

4. Report the output path and total estimated cost.
